- eth_getCode on the networks.json rpc endpoint of each network for the same 61 addresses, 2026-11-26 (out/periphery_code.json) (with PUSH data removed, each unverified copy has the opcode sequence of a verified copy (Universal Router 1.x 55d22501d2fdd506, 2.1.1 055c8f692190c426, 2.1.2 4c62cd3a5abb8da9, PositionManager dcb3d5cf81799507, HyperEVM builds 8def1da8113ecab7 and 6211989b2a1a2b1c), except the router of 21,738 bytes listed on X Layer, MegaETH and Tempo (skeleton 5d8707342b76aa92, the same on all three), which holds one sync(address) selector word and two settle() selector words)

### F-006. Source disagrees with two interface comments and two test conditions, and none of the four changes who can move funds

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: any account. Networks: all 21 networks.

What differs on the network: None. These are differences inside the repository, between src and its own comments and tests.

How it meets the code: First, src/interfaces/IProtocolFees.sol:40 says collectProtocolFees reverts if the contract is unlocked. The code checks only the caller and whether the currency is the synced ERC-20 (src/ProtocolFees.sol:48-52). _isUnlocked is declared at ProtocolFees.sol:60 and implemented at src/PoolManager.sol:391-394 and nothing calls it. The tests agree with the code: they expect ProtocolFeeCurrencySynced in both lock states (test/PoolManager.t.sol:1001-1039). Second, src/interfaces/IPoolManager.sol:111 says only initialize and updateDynamicLPFee can be called without unlocking. sync has no onlyWhenUnlocked modifier (src/PoolManager.sol:279) and test/Sync.t.sol:36-41 calls it while locked. Third, test/libraries/Pool.t.sol:129 expects InvalidFeeForExactOut when amountSpecified >= 0 at a 100 percent fee, while src/libraries/Pool.sol:311-316 reverts only for amountSpecified > 0 and line 320 returns a zero delta for amountSpecified == 0. Fourth, Pool.t.sol:143 treats a zeroForOne price limit below MIN_SQRT_PRICE as out of bounds, while Pool.sol:328 also rejects a limit equal to MIN_SQRT_PRICE. I traced each one for a fund effect. Fee collection inside an unlock changes protocolFeesAccrued and the balance by the same amount and touches no delta. A sync outside an unlock only sets two transient slots that the next sync overwrites. The zero amount swap changes no state. The stricter limit keeps slot0.tick from reaching MIN_TICK - 1.

What is at risk: No loss or freeze found. A reader who trusts the two interface comments gets the wrong picture of when sync and fee collection can run. The fuzz test at Pool.t.sol:129 would fail on an input with amountSpecified equal to zero and a 100 percent fee if the fuzzer produces one.

What it takes: None for the mismatch itself. I could not run forge in this environment, so I do not know whether the current fuzz seed reaches the failing input.

What can be done: The deployer can correct the two comments, decide whether collectProtocolFees should use _isUnlocked or drop it, and align the two test conditions with the source.

Second reviewer's corrections: The fourth item cannot make the test fail. When the limit equals MIN_SQRT_PRICE the test takes neither branch and makes no swap call (test/libraries/Pool.t.sol:135-146), and test/utils/Deployers.sol:43 defines MIN_PRICE_LIMIT as MIN_SQRT_PRICE + 1, which matches the source. The third item is a failing input for the fuzz test whenever the fuzzer picks an LP fee of 1,000,000 together with amountSpecified 0. The setter setIsUnlocked in src/test/ProtocolFeesImplementation.sol:27-29 is also unused by any test, which is a further sign that a lock check on fee collection was planned or removed. ASSUMPTIONS.md A12 words the guard as unlocked and synced. The code applies it whenever the ERC-20 is synced, in both lock states.

Code: `src/ProtocolFees.sol:44-60`, `src/PoolManager.sol:279-288`, `src/PoolManager.sol:391-394`, `src/libraries/Pool.sol:311-330`.

Sources:

- src/interfaces/IProtocolFees.sol:40 (interface comment says collectProtocolFees reverts if the contract is unlocked)
- src/ProtocolFees.sol:48-52 (the only checks are the caller and the synced ERC-20 currency)
- src/interfaces/IPoolManager.sol:111 (interface comment lists initialize and updateDynamicLPFee as the only functions callable while locked)
- test/Sync.t.sol:36-41 (the test calls sync directly while the manager is locked and expects the synced currency to be set)
- test/libraries/Pool.t.sol:129 (test expects InvalidFeeForExactOut for amountSpecified >= 0 at 100 percent fee)
- test/libraries/Pool.t.sol:143 (test uses a strict less than comparison against MIN_SQRT_PRICE)

### F-014. sync runs while the manager is locked and fee collection ignores the lock, and the mainnet fee controller lets anyone trigger collection

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: any account. Networks: Ethereum.

What differs on the network: On mainnet the protocol fee controller is a V4FeeAdapter contract whose collect and triggerFeeUpdate functions have no access check. Any account can therefore make the PoolManager execute collectProtocolFees and setProtocolFee at a moment it chooses, including inside its own unlock callback. Mainnet also lets several users' operations share one transaction through ERC-4337 bundles and EIP-7702 batches.

How it meets the code: sync has no onlyWhenUnlocked modifier (src/PoolManager.sol:279-288), so anyone can set the synced currency and reserves at any point of a transaction, and the value stays until a settle or another sync in that transaction. collectProtocolFees checks the caller and then reverts only if the currency is an ERC-20 equal to the synced currency (src/ProtocolFees.sol:48-52). It never calls _isUnlocked, which is declared at src/ProtocolFees.sol:60 and implemented at src/PoolManager.sol:391-394 with no caller. ASSUMPTIONS.md A12 describes the guard as depending on the unlocked state; the code does not. I walked the fund-moving paths for a gain and found none. Collection of the synced ERC-20 is blocked regardless of lock state, so the balance cannot fall between a sync and its settle through this path. Native settlement uses msg.value and no balance (src/PoolManager.sol:353-354). Collection is bounded by protocolFeesAccrued with checked subtraction (src/ProtocolFees.sol:54-55) and the adapter fixes the recipient. A sync left behind by an earlier operation in a bundle is overwritten by the next sync and cleared by the next ERC-20 settle (src/PoolManager.sol:361). What remains is that a stale sync makes a later native settle with value revert with NonzeroNativeValue if its caller did not sync first (src/PoolManager.sol:356), and makes collection of that currency revert for the rest of the transaction.

What is at risk: No loss or lasting freeze found. Within one transaction, a party that runs earlier can make a later native settle or a fee collection revert.

What it takes: For the revert cases the two parties must share a transaction, for example an ERC-4337 bundle, and the later party must settle native currency without calling sync first. This is my own reading of the code and has not been checked by a second reviewer.

What can be done: Integrators should call sync immediately before every settle, including native, as the comment at src/PoolManager.sol:348 says. The deployer can decide whether sync should require the unlocked state and whether collectProtocolFees should check it, and can correct the A12 text either way.

Second reviewer's corrections: The code facts hold on every network. Only the permissionless controller is specific to mainnet. The repository's own tests expect the present behaviour, so the disagreement is between the code and two texts: the comment at src/interfaces/IProtocolFees.sol:40 and assumption A12. The synced check as written rejects collection in more situations than a check tied to the lock would, so the difference removes no protection.

Code: `src/PoolManager.sol:279-288`, `src/ProtocolFees.sol:44-57`, `src/ProtocolFees.sol:60`, `src/PoolManager.sol:391-394`, `src/PoolManager.sol:349-364`.

Sources:

- https://sourcify.dev/server/v2/contract/1/0x89a5d5bf00a27d55c02951e49078a5c5771051db?fields=sources,abi,compilation (V4FeeAdapter source (exact match): collect(CollectParams[]) and triggerFeeUpdate(PoolKey) have no access modifier and call POOL_MANAGER.collectProtocolFees(TOKEN_JAR, currency, amount) and POOL_MANAGER.setProtocolFee(poolKey, fee))
- https://ethereum-rpc.publicnode.com (eth_call at block 26470380: PoolManager.protocolFeeController() = 0x89a5d5bf00a27d55c02951e49078a5c5771051db; adapter TOKEN_JAR() = 0xf38521f130fccf29db1961597bc5d2b60f995f85; adapter owner() and feeSetter() = timelock)
- https://sourcify.dev/server/v2/contract/1/0xf38521f130fccf29db1961597bc5d2b60f995f85?fields=sources,compilation (TokenJar has an empty receive function and a release function restricted to the releaser, so native fee collection does not hand control to an arbitrary party)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ethereum/ERCs/master/ERCS/erc-4337.md (a bundle is one transaction and transient storage persists across the UserOperations in it)

### F-054. Four interface comments and two test helper remarks disagree with the source, and none of them changes who can move funds

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: any account. Networks: all 21 networks.

What differs on the network: None. These are differences between the source text and the repository's own comments, tests and test doubles. They are the same on every network.

How it meets the code: This unit compared every NatSpec sentence in src/interfaces with the function it describes and read every helper in src/test and test/utils. Six statements do not match the source, in addition to the four recorded in F-006. First, src/interfaces/IHooks.sol:102 says the fee returned by beforeSwap is used only if it is at most 1,000,000. The source reverts with LPFeeTooLarge when the override flag is set and the fee is above the maximum (src/libraries/Pool.sol:303-305, src/libraries/LPFeeLibrary.sol:75-78). The test agrees with the source and expects the revert (test/DynamicReturnFees.t.sol:151-162), although its own comment says the large fee is not used. Second, IHooks.sol:50 and IHooks.sol:81 describe the delta handed to afterAddLiquidity and afterRemoveLiquidity as the sum of principal, fees and hook delta. The source passes principal plus fees, and subtracts the hook delta only after the hook returns (src/PoolManager.sol:171-178, src/libraries/Hooks.sol:219-243). The test doubles rely on the source behaviour (src/test/FeeTakingHook.sol:54-91, test/CustomAccounting.t.sol:270-322). Third, src/interfaces/IPoolManager.sol:85-86 calls the Swap event amounts the deltas of the pool's balances. The emitted values are the swapper's deltas, negative for the amount paid in (src/PoolManager.sol:241-250), and the tests expect -100 and 98 for an exact input of 100 (test/PoolManager.t.sol:500-503). Fourth, IPoolManager.sol:174 says take reverts if the requested amount is not available. That holds for native currency and for tokens with code, and test/PoolManager.t.sol:917-922 states it for an empty manager. For a currency address without code the transfer call succeeds with empty return data and nothing moves (src/types/Currency.sol:60-74), which earlier cycles recorded under assumption A4. Fifth, test/utils/CurrencySettler.sol:20-25 settles native currency without a prior sync and says a sync is not required, while the comment at src/PoolManager.sol:348 tells integrators to call sync before a native settle. A native settle that follows a token sync by other code in the same transaction reverts with NonzeroNativeValue (src/PoolManager.sol:353-356, test/PoolManager.t.sol:976-989). Sixth, src/test/ProtocolFeesImplementation.sol:27-33 offers setIsUnlocked and an _isUnlocked override, no test calls setIsUnlocked, and collectProtocolFees never reads the lock (src/ProtocolFees.sol:44-57). This is the test double side of the first item of F-006. I traced each item for a fund effect. A hook that returns an oversized fee can only stop swaps in its own pool. The delta seen by a liquidity hook is the amount the hook can charge against, and the caller delta plus the hook delta still equals the pool delta in every replayed case. The event wording affects readers of logs only. The native settle revert needs code that runs earlier in the same transaction, and it returns the caller's value with the revert.

What is at risk: No loss or freeze found. A hook author who trusts IHooks.sol:102 may ship a hook that makes swaps in its own pool revert when it computes a fee above 100 percent. An integrator that copies the native branch of the test helper without sync(address(0)) can have a native settle reverted by a token sync left earlier in the same transaction.

What it takes: None for the comments. The native settle revert needs a call to sync with a token address earlier in the same transaction, for example from a hook of a pool the caller used or from a contract that bundles several actions.

What can be done: Correct the six texts. Hook authors should cap the fee before setting the override flag. Integrators should call sync(address(0)) before every native settle, as the comment at src/PoolManager.sol:348 says.

Second reviewer's corrections: No correction to the six items. Two precisions. The fourth item says the comment holds for tokens with code. It holds only for tokens whose transfer reverts or returns false on a shortfall, because Currency.sol:68 also accepts empty return data from a contract with code. The actor value is nominal here. The record describes no loss or freeze. The two operational effects need a hook author for the fee revert and a party that runs earlier in the same transaction for the native settle revert, which F-014 already records.

Code: `src/libraries/Pool.sol:303-305`, `src/libraries/Hooks.sol:209-245`, `src/PoolManager.sol:171-178`, `src/PoolManager.sol:241-250`, `src/PoolManager.sol:348-365`, `src/ProtocolFees.sol:44-60`, `src/types/Currency.sol:60-74`.

Sources:

- src/interfaces/IHooks.sol:102 (Comment says an override fee above the maximum is not used.)
- src/libraries/Pool.sol:303-305 (The source validates the override fee and reverts with LPFeeTooLarge when it is above 1,000,000.)
- test/DynamicReturnFees.t.sol:151-162 (The test expects LPFeeTooLarge for an override fee of 1,000,001.)
- src/interfaces/IHooks.sol:50 (Comment says the delta passed to afterAddLiquidity includes the hook delta. Line 81 says the same for removal.)
- src/libraries/Hooks.sol:219-243 (The hook receives the delta before the hook delta is subtracted.)
- src/interfaces/IPoolManager.sol:85-86 (Comment describes the Swap event amounts as pool balance deltas.)
- test/PoolManager.t.sol:500-503 (The test expects amount0 -100 and amount1 98 for an exact input of 100 of currency0.)
- test/utils/CurrencySettler.sol:20-25 (The helper settles native currency without calling sync first.)
- src/PoolManager.sol:348 (Comment says integrators should call sync before a native settle.)
- src/test/ProtocolFeesImplementation.sol:27-33 (The test double has a lock toggle that no test uses.)

### F-055. Two more places where the repository's tests and test doubles disagree with the library source, and neither changes who can move funds

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: any account. Networks: all 21 networks.

What differs on the network: None. The disagreements are between files of this repository and hold on every network.

How it meets the code: This extends F-006 with what a replay of test/libraries, test/Tick.t.sol, test/types and the test doubles in src/test found. First, test/libraries/Pool.t.sol:73-83 expects SafeCastOverflow from Pool.modifyLiquidity only when a token amount is above 2^128 - 1. The variable is named maxInt128InTypeU256 and holds Constants.MAX_UINT128. The source casts both amounts to int128: Pool.sol:212-235 takes the signed results of SqrtPriceMath.sol:261-288 and SafeCast.toInt128 (SafeCast.sol:40-43) reverts once the amount of an add is above 2^127. For amounts from 2^127 + 1 to 2^128 - 1 the source reverts and the test expects success. One case from the replay: spacing 32767, price 735853766953812733719635225319847024411115828962 (tick 873548), range -131068 to 98301, liquidity 2300850342447660200206975412810193765, which is below the per tick cap 6076470837873901133274546561281575204. The amount of currency1 is 313585663563401618519606321096647269736 and the source reverts with SafeCastOverflow. Out of 400,000 random parameter sets 346 fell in this window. Every other outcome, including the order of the eight revert conditions at Pool.t.sol:51-70, matched the source. Second, src/test/SqrtPriceMathEchidnaTest.sol:144-197 asserts that the signed helpers return a positive amount for positive liquidity and a non-positive amount for negative liquidity. The source returns minus the rounded up amount for positive liquidity and the rounded down amount for negative liquidity (SqrtPriceMath.sol:266-270 and 283-287). On 200,000 random inputs the assertion for positive liquidity fails 135,655 times and the one for negative liquidity 48,354 times as written, and every other invariant of that file holds. The forge tests use the sign of the source: a negative delta is owed by the caller (PoolManager.sol:183 and 368-378), and test/libraries/StateLibrary.t.sol:300-301 negates the delta of an add to get the amount paid. The echidna harness is not part of forge test, so nothing in the repository runs it. Third, a correction to the fourth item of F-006: test/libraries/Pool.t.sol:135-158 calls swap for a nonzero amount only when it expects a revert. It makes no call for a valid limit and no call when a zeroForOne limit equals MIN_SQRT_PRICE, so that file says nothing about the boundary that Pool.sol:328-330 rejects and contains no successful nonzero swap. The third item of F-006 was replayed: 8,795 of 120,000 random cases had a zero amount with a 100 percent swap fee, where Pool.t.sol:129-131 expects InvalidFeeForExactOut and Pool.sol:311-320 returns a zero delta without touching state.

What is at risk: None on funds. In the first case the source refuses an add that the test would let through, so it is stricter than the test. A removal whose amount is 2^127 or more reverts at the same cast and has to be split, which an earlier cleared item on the int128 casts already covers. In the second case the source sign is the one the accounting in PoolManager needs: with the sign of the echidna file an add would credit the caller.

What it takes: None. No account gains anything from these disagreements.

What can be done: Change the bound at test/libraries/Pool.t.sol:73 to the int128 maximum and bring the sign assertions in src/test/SqrtPriceMathEchidnaTest.sol in line with SqrtPriceMath, so that the tests and the harness can serve as a reference for the source.

This entry adds to F-006.

Second reviewer's corrections: No party gains or loses through these disagreements, so the actor value carries no weight here; unprivileged is the value the existing F-006 entry already has. The counts 346 of 400,000, 135,655 and 48,354 could not be reproduced because the finding does not state its sampling distribution; the direction of each result reproduces. The third part repeats a correction that the cycle 1 verification of F-006 already recorded. The statement that nothing in the repository runs the echidna harness needs one addition: .github/workflows/mythx.yml:60 lists SqrtPriceMathEchidnaTest under contracts/test/, a directory that does not exist in this repository, and echidna.config.yml is present. No forge test runs the harness. On the limit for an add: an amount of exactly 2^127 passes, because the amount is negated before the cast and -2^127 fits int128. A removal passes up to 2^127 - 1.

Code: `src/libraries/Pool.sol:206-237`, `src/libraries/SqrtPriceMath.sol:261-288`, `src/libraries/SafeCast.sol:40-43`, `src/libraries/Pool.sol:311-330`.

Sources:

- test/libraries/Pool.t.sol:73-83 (The test expects SafeCastOverflow only above Constants.MAX_UINT128.)
- src/libraries/Pool.sol:212-235 (Both amounts of a liquidity change are cast with toInt128.)
- src/libraries/SafeCast.sol:40-43 (toInt128(int256) reverts when the value does not fit in int128.)
- src/test/SqrtPriceMathEchidnaTest.sol:144-197 (The echidna harness asserts a positive amount for positive liquidity.)
- src/libraries/SqrtPriceMath.sol:261-288 (The signed helpers return a negative amount for positive liquidity, rounded up, and a positive amount for negative liquidity, rounded down.)
- test/libraries/Pool.t.sol:129-158 (The swap fuzz test calls swap for nonzero amounts only when it expects a revert.)
- test/libraries/StateLibrary.t.sol:300-301 (The forge tests treat the delta of an add as negative.)

### F-100. The two lock guards that Trail of Bits finding 3 records as its fix are absent from the source, and the synced currency check that stands in their place skips address(0), which shares its balance with an ERC-20 address on five networks

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: any account. Networks: Ethereum, Optimism, Base, Arbitrum One, Robinhood Chain, Polygon, BNB Smart Chain, Arc.

What differs on the network: On Ethereum no ERC-20 address reports the PoolManager's native balance. On five of the 21 networks one does: LegacyERC20ETH at 0xDeadDeAddeAddEAddeadDEaDDEAdDeaDDeAD0000 on Optimism and Zora, 0x0000000000000000000000000000000000001010 on Polygon, the CELO token 0x471EcE3750Da237f93B8E339c536989b8978a438 on Celo and 0x3600000000000000000000000000000000000000 on Arc, where the figure is the native balance divided by 10^12. I read balanceOf(PoolManager) and eth_getBalance at one block per network and they agree on these five and on no candidate address of the other 16. Optimism, Polygon and Arc also have a fee controller whose collect function any account can call. Celo and Zora have no fee controller today.

How it meets the code: 1. The report (printed page 33) describes the hazard: a fee collection between sync and settle lowers the balance, so the settle that follows credits less or reverts. Its fix review (printed page 62) records the fix as two guards: sync can be called only while the manager is unlocked, and collectProtocolFees reverts with ContractUnlocked while the manager is unlocked. 2. The source has neither guard. sync carries no modifier (src/PoolManager.sol:279-288). collectProtocolFees checks the caller and then reverts only when the currency is nonzero and equals the synced currency (src/ProtocolFees.sol:48-52). _isUnlocked is declared at src/ProtocolFees.sol:60 and implemented at src/PoolManager.sol:392-394 and has no caller. The interface still says the function reverts if the contract is unlocked (src/interfaces/IProtocolFees.sol:40) and declares ProtocolFeeCurrencySynced and no ContractUnlocked error (line 17). The tests of the repository expect the source behaviour in both lock states (test/PoolManager.t.sol:1001-1039) and call sync while locked (test/Sync.t.sol:36-41). 3. The check that replaced the guards closes the report's case when the collected id is the synced id. It compares ids and exempts address(0) with the comment that native settle uses msg.value. That reasoning assumes the native balance has no second id (A3). 4. Where a native face exists and a controller can be triggered, the report's sequence runs again across two ids: sync(face), collection of native fees, settle. My walk on the deployed PoolManagers of Optimism (block 159,929,377), Polygon (97,834,382) and Arc (32,774,161), with a stub placed at the controller address by state override, gave the same three answers on each: collection of the face id reverted with ProtocolFeeCurrencySynced, collection of address(0) succeeded inside the unlock while the face was synced and paid out the accrued native fees (0.0407 ETH, 714.3 POL and 53.28 USDC), and the settle that followed reverted with a Panic from the checked subtraction at src/PoolManager.sol:360. On the other five controller networks the same walk with a stub token that returns the native balance of its argument gave the same answers. 5. A separate eth_call of collect((address,uint256)[]) with one entry for address(0) from an arbitrary account succeeded on all eight controllers, and each controller names one fixed TOKEN_JAR. 6. I traced who pays. Collection lowers protocolFeesAccrued by a checked subtraction before the transfer (src/ProtocolFees.sol:54-56), writes no delta and no transient slot, and pays the recipient the controller names. The lower balance reduces the credit of the account that holds the face bracket in that same transaction, or reverts its settle. A payer that syncs, transfers and settles without calling other code in between cannot be interleaved. The caller of collect gains nothing. 7. Two further effects follow from the absent guards. Any code that runs earlier in a shared transaction can leave sync(X) and make a later collection of X revert (src/ProtocolFees.sol:49-51), which contradicts the report's invariant UNI-ACTION-6 as worded (collectProtocolFees must not revert on valid input). The same stale sync makes a later native settle with value revert, which the ledger holds as F-071.

What is at risk: No loss of pool funds and no lasting freeze found. The account that syncs a native face and lets a native fee collection run before its settle loses credit equal to the collected amount or has its transaction reverted. Fee collection of a currency can be made to revert by an earlier sync in the same transaction. The finding extends F-014 from Ethereum to the eight networks with a controller and ties it to the audit record.

What it takes: A fee controller that any account can trigger (Ethereum, Optimism, Base, Arbitrum One, Robinhood Chain, Polygon, BNB Smart Chain and Arc today). For the two id case also an address that reports the native balance (Optimism, Polygon and Arc today, and Celo and Zora once the owner sets a controller there). The affected party must itself hold a sync of the face while the collection runs.

What can be done: Restore the lock check that the interface comment describes, or make the synced check cover address(0) as well. Contracts that harvest fees and settle in one transaction should collect before they sync. Routers should call sync themselves before each payment, native payments included.

This entry adds to F-014.

Second reviewer's corrections: The evidence for an open collect function was a call that did not revert. The Multicall3 run above shows the payout and should be cited in its place. The finding should say in its impact field that every on-chain test describes the deployed contracts and that the checkout was not compiled. On each tested point the deployed contracts gave the answer that the cited source lines predict. Severity info stands. Low would also fit the scale for the integration hazard, and the ledger already holds the stale sync part as F-071 at low.

Code: `src/ProtocolFees.sol:44-60`, `src/PoolManager.sol:279-288`, `src/PoolManager.sol:349-365`, `src/PoolManager.sol:391-394`, `src/interfaces/IProtocolFees.sol:13-17`, `src/interfaces/IProtocolFees.sol:39-47`.

Sources:

- docs/security/audits/TrailOfBits_audit_core.pdf:33 (Printed page 33, finding 3: collection between sync and settle counts against the settler, and the short term recommendation is a guard on collectProtocolFees while unlocked and a guard on sync)
- docs/security/audits/TrailOfBits_audit_core.pdf:62 (Printed page 62, fix review: a guard was added to sync so that it can only be called when unlocked, and collectProtocolFees reverts with ContractUnlocked when unlocked)
- src/ProtocolFees.sol:48 (Lines 48 to 52 check the caller and the synced ERC-20 only, address(0) is exempt, and no lock is read)
- src/PoolManager.sol:279 (sync has no onlyWhenUnlocked modifier)
- test/PoolManager.t.sol:1001 (Lines 1001 to 1039: the tests expect ProtocolFeeCurrencySynced with the manager locked and unlocked)
- eth_call with state overrides on the rpc URL of each of the 21 entries of research/network-risk/state/networks.json on 2026-11-23, script walk.py and results raw/walk.json in research/network-risk/cycles/007/core-code-audit-walk-a.work (On Optimism, Polygon and Arc a native fee collection succeeded inside an unlock while the real native face was synced and the next settle reverted with Panic; on all eight controller networks the same held with a stub face; sync succeeded while locked on all 21)
- eth_call of collect((address,uint256)[]) from 0xe0a0000000000000000000000000000000000e0a on the rpc URLs of chain ids 1, 10, 8453, 42161, 4663, 137, 56 and 5042 on 2026-11-23, script collect.py, results raw/collect.json in the work directory of this unit (collect succeeds for an arbitrary caller on all eight fee controllers, and TOKEN_JAR() returns one fixed address on each)
- eth_getBalance and eth_call balanceOf at one block per network on the 21 rpc URLs on 2026-11-23, script faces.py, results raw/faces.json in the work directory of this unit (The five addresses that report the PoolManager's native balance, and the absence of such an answer from the same candidates on the other 16 networks)

### F-101. OpenZeppelin CR-01 records that noDelegateCall was added to all relevant external functions, and the source carries it on the four pool functions only

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: any account. Networks: all 21 networks.

What differs on the network: None found. The guard compares address(this) with an immutable (src/NoDelegateCall.sol:24-26). Earlier cycles tested on all 21 deployed PoolManagers that a DELEGATECALL into the unguarded functions uses the storage, transient storage and balance of the calling account, and on 20 networks that an EIP-7702 account that names the PoolManager behaves the same. The Avalanche endpoint did not resolve a delegation designator.

How it meets the code: 1. The report (page 24) says the modifier on unlock was meant to stop proxies for licence reasons, that a custom unlock that writes the same lock slot got around it, and that the team added noDelegateCall to all relevant external functions. 2. In the source the modifier sits on initialize, modifyLiquidity, swap and donate (src/PoolManager.sol:117, 148, 190, 259). unlock, sync, take, settle, settleFor, clear, mint and burn carry none (lines 104, 279, 291, 300, 305, 310, 322, 332). The test double of the repository puts it on unlock, take, settle, settleFor, mint and burn (src/test/ProxyPoolManager.sol:58, 151, 156, 163, 176, 181), and test/NoDelegateCall.t.sol:63-82 expects the revert from modifyLiquidity, swap and donate only. 3. I checked whether the gap reaches funds. A delegatecall runs the settlement functions on the lock flag, deltas, claims and balance of the calling contract. No pool can be created or used that way, because all four pool functions revert with DelegateCallNotAllowed. The PoolManager itself contains no delegatecall (search of src outside src/test). The stated purpose of the fix, keeping pool logic from running behind a proxy, is met by the four guarded functions. 4. The cleared items of cycles 1, 3 and 6 reach the same result, the last one by a test on every network.

What is at risk: No effect on funds held by the PoolManager. A contract or delegated account that runs this code on itself exposes its own balances to flash use and to the booking of balance increases, which concerns only that account.

What it takes: None for the gap itself. An effect needs an account that chooses to run PoolManager code in its own context.

What can be done: Add the modifier to the six functions that the test double guards if the licence purpose is to cover the settlement layer as well. No change is needed for custody.

Second reviewer's corrections: State that the report names no list of functions. The rest stands. Actor unprivileged is acceptable for an info entry, because the only account exposed is one that chooses to run this code in its own context.

Code: `src/PoolManager.sol:104-114`, `src/PoolManager.sol:279-336`, `src/NoDelegateCall.sol:14-32`.

Sources:

- docs/security/audits/OpenZeppelin_audit_core.pdf:24 (CR-01: the limitation can be skipped, and the update says noDelegateCall was added to all relevant external functions)
- src/PoolManager.sol:117 (Lines 117, 148, 190 and 259 are the only uses of the modifier)
- src/test/ProxyPoolManager.sol:58 (The test double guards unlock, take, settle, settleFor, mint and burn at lines 58, 151, 156, 163, 176 and 181)
- research/network-risk/state/cleared.json: core-code-loose-guards, cycle 6 (DELEGATECALL into the unguarded functions on all 21 deployed PoolManagers used the caller's storage and balance, and delegated accounts gave the same answers on 20 networks)

### F-125. Arc wraps every value-carrying CALL in a frame hook that fails transfers to address(0), to blocklisted accounts and to accounts destroyed earlier in the transaction, which changes the success flag of a native take

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: any account. Networks: Arc.

What differs on the network: Ethereum executes CALL with value to any address and only fails it for insufficient balance or depth. arc-node v0.8.6 intercepts frame initialisation (ArcEvm::frame_init, crates/evm/src/evm.rs lines 669-711) and, for every CALL or CREATE that carries a nonzero value, runs before_frame_init (lines 607-629) and check_blocklist_and_create_log (lines 516-605). That function returns a revert with an Error(string) payload when the sender or the recipient is address(0) ("Zero address not allowed"), when either is set in the NativeCoinControl blocklist ("Blocked address"), or when the recipient was selfdestructed earlier in the transaction. On success it adds an EIP-7708 Transfer log. The blocklist reads are journal SLOADs that are not charged (meter_sloads = false at line 525), so a rejected frame returns all of its gas. The only replaced instruction handler is SELFDESTRUCT (insert_arc_selfdestruct_instruction, lines 114-134). Calls to 0x1800000000000000000000000000000000000003 (CallFrom) are diverted to a subcall precompile that admits only two callers (lines 1805-1820) and charges 100 gas to anyone else (lines 137-151).

How it meets the code: 1. take(address(0), to, amount) with amount > 0 makes the PoolManager run call(gas(), to, amount, 0, 0, 0, 0) at src/types/Currency.sol:48. 2. On Arc the frame hook sees a value transfer from the PoolManager to `to`. With to = address(0) it returns Revert with 100 bytes of Error(string). eth_call from the PoolManager address measured success 0 and the reason string on Arc, and success 1 on the 13 other networks tested, Ethereum included (raw/zero-recipient.json). With amount = 0 the hook is skipped and the call succeeds as on Ethereum. 3. The PoolManager sees success = false and reverts through CustomRevert.bubbleUpAndRevertWith with NativeTransferFailed (src/types/Currency.sol:51-52), which also undoes the debit booked at src/PoolManager.sol:294. 4. The recipient is the `to` argument that the caller of take passes (src/PoolManager.sol:291-297). In a swap or removal on an honest pool the PoolManager makes outbound calls only to the pool's two currencies and its hook, which are fixed by the PoolKey that the pool id hashes (src/PoolManager.sol:132, 151), to the unlock caller (src/PoolManager.sol:110) and to the take recipient. None of these is chosen by a third party, so an attacker cannot place address(0), a selfdestructed account or a blocklisted account into a victim's take. Blocklisting is done by the USDC issuer (F-108).

What is at risk: No funds of other users are affected. A caller that asks for a native payout to address(0) gets a revert on Arc where Ethereum would burn the value, so the caller keeps its credit. A router that encodes address(0) as a burn sink would revert on Arc. The selfdestructed-target rule can only be met by a contract that the caller itself destroyed in its own transaction.

What it takes: The caller of take chooses the recipient. No role is needed, and no third party can impose the recipient on someone else's transaction.

What can be done: None needed in core. Integrators on Arc should not use address(0) as a native burn recipient.

This entry adds to F-108.

Code: `src/types/Currency.sol:44-52`, `src/PoolManager.sol:291-297`, `src/PoolManager.sol:110`, `src/PoolManager.sol:132`, `src/libraries/Hooks.sol:134`.

Sources:

- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/circlefin/arc-node/v0.8.6/crates/evm/src/evm.rs (lines 114-134 replace only SELFDESTRUCT; lines 516-605 reject value transfers involving address(0), blocklisted accounts or selfdestructed targets and add an EIP-7708 log; line 525 leaves the blocklist SLOADs unmetered; lines 669-711 route frame initialisation through the hook and the subcall registry; lines 1805-1820 register CallFrom at Zero7 with two allowed callers)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/circlefin/arc-node/v0.8.6/crates/evm/src/opcode.rs (SELFDESTRUCT variants for the three hardfork levels are the only instruction functions in the file (lines 64-200))
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/circlefin/arc-node/v0.8.6/crates/precompiles/src/helpers.rs (line 57 ERR_ZERO_ADDRESS text, line 82 a 200 gas penalty for early reverts, lines 540-555 check_staticcall spends all gas)
- eth_call https://rpc.mainnet.arc.io from 0x...0f0f0f to the Arc PoolManager 0x8366a39CC670B4001A1121B8F6A443A643e40951 with driver code override: CALL value 1 to address(0) (success 0, 34,329 gas, 100 byte Error(string) with a 24 character reason; value 0 succeeds; the same call succeeds on Ethereum, Tempo, MegaETH, Monad, HyperEVM, Arbitrum One, Base, OP Mainnet, BNB Smart Chain, Polygon, Avalanche, Celo and X Layer (research/network-risk/cycles/013/theme-callee-gas-and-overrides.work/raw/zero-recipient.json))
- src/types/Currency.sol:48 (native payout forwards gas() and value to the caller-supplied recipient)
- src/PoolManager.sol:291-297 (take pays the recipient named by its caller after debiting the caller)

### F-127. On Base any account with a valid SP1 proof can challenge a TEE-only proposal or nullify a verifier for all games, and the range program computes the opcodes the PoolManager uses as the node does

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: freeze. Who can trigger it: any account. Networks: Base.

What differs on the network: Base settles withdrawals on game type 621, AggregateVerifier 0.3.0 at 0xc08b8ffa...5a70, with PROOF_THRESHOLD 1, TEE_IMAGE_HASH 0x73181275...5cbd, ZK_RANGE_HASH 0x3d3713d3...5fdb and ZK_AGGREGATE_HASH 0x00d5a430...d45a (eth_call on Ethereum at block 26,487,041). L2BEAT ties both program hashes to base/base commit 6efa7566. The range program at that commit pins revm 42.0.1, revm-interpreter 42.0.0, ruint 1.18.0 and alloy-primitives 1.6.1 and is built for the SP1 riscv64im zkVM target, while the node and TEE workspace at the same commit pins ruint 1.19.0. The Base proof specification lets challengers call challenge() with a ZK proof for one intermediate root, and lets nullify() with contradicting proof material delete an accepted proof and disable that verifier for every later game. TEE proofs need an allowlisted proposer; the specification names no allowlist for ZK proofs or challenges.

How it meets the code: If the SP1 range program computed an opcode differently from the node, an ordinary account could post a transaction that hits the input, build an SP1 proof of the program's root for that range, and call challenge() on the honest TEE-backed game or nullify() on its TEE proof. Nullification disables the TEE verifier for all games until Base reconfigures it, which delays every withdrawal, including bridged pool funds. With a proof threshold of 1 a ZK-only proposal of the program's root would also resolve alone. The check: the revm-interpreter 42.0.0 handlers (identical to 43.0.1 for every arithmetic, comparison and shift opcode) over ruint 1.18.0 were compiled with rustc 1.97.0 for riscv64 with the M extension and run in an RV64IM emulator written from the RISC-V specification. All 85,544 targeted and 96,876 battery vectors, EXP included, give the specified word, and 3,794 mulDiv and bit scan cases and 2,283 ticks match; planted faults in MULHU and in the shift mask are caught. No call into compiler-builtins appears in the arithmetic.

What is at risk: No divergence found, so no loss or freeze path. The finding records that on Base a divergence of the zkVM build would be usable by an unprivileged account, unlike Celo and X Layer where challengers are allowlisted.

What it takes: A divergence between the SP1 range program and the Base node for some EVM input. None was found for the arithmetic, comparison and shift opcodes. The SP1 circuits themselves, the RISC-V toolchain Succinct ships and keccak 0.2.0 on the zkVM were not tested.

What can be done: Keep the range program and the node on one revm and ruint version, and rerun the vector set through each new range program build before its hash is set.

Second reviewer's corrections: The test ran a rustc 1.97 rebuild of the crates on an RV64IM emulator, not the published SP1 ELF under Succinct's toolchain and circuits; the finding says so in its preconditions. The actor unprivileged describes who could use a divergence if one existed; with none found the record is info.

Code: `src/libraries/FullMath.sol:24-48`, `src/libraries/BitMath.sol:16-22`, `src/libraries/TickMath.sol:63-111`, `src/PoolManager.sol:291-297`.

Sources:

- https://docs.base.org/specifications/base-protocol/proofs/proof-contracts.md (challenge() and nullify() semantics, global verifier nullification, TEE proposer allowlist, permissionless resolve().)
- https://l2beat.com/scaling/projects/base (Program hashes map to base/base commit 6efa75662b57c578d897fea2449035636f6ad9ad; any party with a valid ZK proof can challenge (fetched with Exa via ethbeat.org mirror).)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/base/base/6efa75662b57c578d897fea2449035636f6ad9ad/crates/proof/zk/programs/succinct/Cargo.lock (Range program pins revm 42.0.1, revm-interpreter 42.0.0, ruint 1.18.0, alloy-primitives 1.6.1 with sha3 0.11.0.)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/base/base/6efa75662b57c578d897fea2449035636f6ad9ad/crates/proof/zk/programs/succinct/Cargo.toml (SP1 6.10.0, sp1-patches for tiny-keccak, sha2, sha3 0.10.8 and curves, riscv64im-succinct-zkvm-elf toolchain.)
- https://ethereum-rpc.publicnode.com (eth_call at block 26,487,041: DGF 0x43edb88c gameImpls(621) = 0xc08b8ffa, PROOF_THRESHOLD 1, TEE and ZK verifiers not nullified, ASR 0x909f6cf4 respectedGameType 621 (research/network-risk/cycles/013/theme-proof-builds-2.work/base/aggverifier.json, celo/dgf.json, celo/access.json).)
- file:research/network-risk/cycles/013/theme-proof-builds-2.work/base/base-rv64-vectors.json (0 mismatches on the RV64IM build; planted faults caught.)

### F-133. Follower nodes on OP Mainnet, Base, Zora, World Chain, Celo, X Layer and reth-bsc validate blocks through an execution cache with the stale destroyed-account branch

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: any account. Networks: Optimism, Base, Zora, Worldchain, Celo, X Layer, BNB Smart Chain.

What differs on the network: The builds the public endpoints report pin reth revisions with the old branch: op-reth v2.8.1 (OP Mainnet), conduit-op-reth v2.5.1 (Zora) and world-chain v2.8.2 pin ethereum-optimism/reth op-v2.6.0-2 (3c5f2f1, cached_state.rs lines 1286-1305); Base pins base/reth 9e380dd (lines 1278-1297); Celo pins celo-org/reth 74cc8bb (lines 863-882); X Layer v0.0.7 pins okx/reth 81573d2 (lines 904-923); reth-bsc v0.1.4-hotfix pins bnb-chain/reth 3b2bd02 (lines 863-882). Engine tree validation of newPayload reads through this cache by default on all of them.

How it meets the code: On a follower, a stale word from the branch is read while the node re-executes a canonical block. A different read makes the state root, receipts root or gas used differ from the header, so the node rejects a valid block and stops following until restarted, because the cache is in memory. It does not commit wrong state. The same accounts as in the producing-path finding are the only ones that can be read stale, so no read made by the PoolManager, or by a token, hook, router, PositionManager or Permit2 that existed before the block, changes (src/PoolManager.sol:279-288, 349-364). The trigger needs a cached word for an address without code before the block that destroys and re-creates it; I could not trace how an ordinary block produces that word on these builds.

What is at risk: No PoolManager funds move. A stalled RPC node shows an old head to the users and integrations that read from it.

What it takes: A node operator runs one of the listed builds as a validating follower, and the trigger condition above holds.

What can be done: Operators upgrade to a base with reth pull request 28148. Integrations compare block heads across two endpoints before acting.

This entry adds to F-124.

Rated low and plausible until cycle 17. The cycle 17 reading of celo-reth, X Layer reth v0.0.7 and reth-bsc, confirmed by its verifier, finds no default path that stores a storage word of an address without code in the cache, the same result cycle 16 reached for revm 42.0.0. Rated info and confirmed: the stale branch exists, and nothing seeds it.

Second reviewer's corrections: actor: unprivileged. The followers run default software, so any account could send the trigger transactions if the seeding link exists, and no operator needs to act. The effect is still an RPC follower stall with no path to PoolManager funds, so the finding stays out of scope for funds. Severity is low (an operational hazard) rather than info. The OP Mainnet endpoint returned only the v0.0.0-dev-unknown build on my query, so its pin to op-v2.6.0-2 is not confirmed for current requests.

Code: `src/PoolManager.sol:279-288`, `src/PoolManager.sol:349-364`.

Sources:

- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ethereum-optimism/reth/3c5f2f11c4e5a5647e6e911cd131ccf1bada4f25/crates/engine/execution-cache/src/cached_state.rs (lines 1286-1305: old branch in ethereum-optimism/reth op-v2.6.0-2)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/okx/reth/81573d2c7d806d5df9ff2d512dd000c2bf542b1b/crates/engine/execution-cache/src/cached_state.rs (lines 904-923: old branch in okx/reth 81573d2)

### F-139. Under Jenner BNB Chain routes two CAS20 token ranges and three fixed addresses to native code, an address there with no token reverts every call, and no pool can hold funds in such a currency

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: any account. Networks: BNB Smart Chain.

What differs on the network: From 2026-12-08 02:30 UTC (JennerTime 1796697000 in params/config.go line 256 at v1.8.2) BNB Smart Chain routes an address to native CAS20 code when bytes 0 and 1 are 0xCA52, bytes 2 to 9 are zero and byte 10 is 0x00 (Asset) or 0x01 (Stablecoin), and also routes the factory 0xCA5F00..00 and the registries 0x7020..01 and 0x7020..02. A token address whose code is not the one byte marker 0xEF reverts every call. Addresses in the same space with byte 10 from 0x02 to 0xff are ordinary accounts. The time precompile 0x70 answers any call with the block time in milliseconds. Ethereum has none of these. The cycle 19 text of this finding said every address in the CAS20 space reverts; that holds only for the two ordinals and the fixed addresses.

How it meets the code: core/vm/evm.go lines 46-59 (v1.8.2) first consults the static precompile map and, when IsJenner holds, calls resolveCAS20. resolveCAS20 (core/vm/cas20.go lines 122-135) returns the three singletons by exact address and otherwise, for an address that passes IsCAS20Address (bytes 2 to 9 zero, lines 50-60), looks up cas20Variants[addr[10]] (lines 107-119), which has entries only for 0x00 and 0x01. For any other ordinal the lookup returns ok = false and EVM.Call treats the address as an ordinary account (evm.go lines 318-360). For a routed token address runCAS20Token reverts unless the code hash equals keccak256(0xEF) (cas20.go lines 62-70 and 189-200; cas20_factory.go line 35). The cycle 19 probe that F-139 and lead L-907 rest on called 0xca52...0001, 0xca52...0000 and 0xca52...00ff, and all three have byte 10 equal to 0x00, so the reverts it saw for 'byte 10 = 0x02 or 0xff' were in fact ordinal 0x00 addresses. This cycle called byte 10 in {0x00, 0x01, 0x02, 0xff} on four endpoints with web3_clientVersion before and after each batch: ordinals 0x00 and 0x01 revert under the block time override 0x6b176e00 and ordinals 0x02 and 0xff return empty success, as the tag says, on Geth v1.8.2-4e1c8247 (dataseed and dataseed1), Geth v1.8.2-9b93ce23 and v1.8.2-132104cd (48 Club answered both builds in one batch) and reth-bsc v0.1.4-hotfix-23b510f on every cell it answered. For the PoolManager the effect is the one F-139 describes: for a routed address X with no token, take(X, to, 0) succeeds and moves nothing today and reverts after the fork (src/types/Currency.sol:64-86); sync(X) reverts before and after because the high level balanceOf call needs 32 bytes of return data (src/types/Currency.sol:90-96, src/PoolManager.sol:279-288). For an unrouted ordinal nothing changes at the fork: take succeeds and moves nothing, sync reverts. With no sync there is no settle credit (src/PoolManager.sol:349-364), so no delta, claim or liquidity in such an X can exist.

What is at risk: None. No pool can be funded in a CAS20 space currency that has no token, before or after the fork, and the fork changes nothing for the unrouted ordinals.

What it takes: Jenner activates as scheduled. Token creation stays closed until a governance appointed administrator enables it (F-076). A token address can only become a token through the factory, because the marker code 0xEF cannot be deployed by CREATE or CREATE2 (EIP-3541) and bytes 0 to 9 fix 80 bits of the address.

What can be done: None needed. Integrators that cache pools by currency should not assume that a routed address which answered as an empty account before the fork keeps doing so.

This entry adds to F-076.

Code: `src/types/Currency.sol:64-86`, `src/types/Currency.sol:90-96`, `src/PoolManager.sol:279-288`, `src/PoolManager.sol:349-364`.

Sources:

- https://github.com/bnb-chain/bsc/releases (v1.8.2 schedules Jenner at 1796697000 with BEP-702 CAS20 precompiles, BEP-703 and the 0x70 precompile of BEP-706)
- rpc: eth_call with blockOverrides time 0x6b176e00 on bsc-dataseed1.bnbchain.org, rpc-bsc.48.club and bsc-rpc.publicnode.com, 2026-11-25, research/network-risk/cycles/019/established-l1.work/raw/cas20_probe.json (CAS20-range addresses revert every call shape under Jenner rules and answer empty success today; 0x70 returns 0x1a25385b000)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/bnb-chain/bsc/v1.8.2/core/vm/cas20.go (IsCAS20Address checks bytes 0-1 = 0xCA52 and bytes 2-9 zero (lines 50-60); resolveCAS20Token indexes cas20Variants by byte 10 with entries only for 0x00 and 0x01 (lines 107-119); resolveCAS20 adds the three singletons (lines 122-135); runCAS20Token reverts unless the code hash is that of the marker (lines 62-70, 189-200); DisabledPrecompile makes dispatch treat the address as an ordinary account (lines 38-48))
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/bnb-chain/bsc/v1.8.2/core/vm/evm.go (EVM.precompile consults the map, returns not-a-precompile for DisabledPrecompile, and routes CAS20 only when IsJenner holds (lines 46-67); EVM.Call runs a precompile or the account's code (lines 299-360))
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/bnb-chain/bsc/v1.8.2/core/vm/cas20_factory.go (CAS20MarkerCode is the single byte 0xEF (line 35) and the factory writes it at token creation (lines 161-164))
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/bnb-chain/bsc/v1.8.2/params/config.go (BSC mainnet JennerTime 1796697000, 2026-12-08 02:30 UTC (line 256))
- rpc: eth_call and eth_estimateGas from the PoolManager to CAS20 space addresses with byte 10 in {0x00,0x01,0x02,0xff}, singletons and 0x70, with and without blockOverrides time 0x6b176e00 and with and without a code override, on bsc-dataseed1.bnbchain.org, bsc-dataseed.bnbchain.org, rpc-bsc.48.club and bsc-rpc.publicnode.com, 2026-11-25, research/network-risk/cycles/020/theme-reserved-address-routing.work/raw/cas20_probe.json and raw/cas20_reth_retry.json (ordinals 0x00 and 0x01 and the singletons revert under the override, ordinals 0x02 and 0xff answer empty success with gas 21000, all four builds agree on every answered cell; publicnode refused part of the calls with a Cloudflare challenge (HTTP 403), recorded as refusals)
- rpc: eth_call on https://bsc-dataseed1.bnbchain.org, 2026-11-25, from 0x28e2ea090877bf75740558f6bfb36a5ffee9e9df, balanceOf calldata, blockOverrides time 0x6b176e00 and none (byte 10 = 0x00, 0x01 and the factory revert under the override; byte 10 = 0x02 and 0xff return empty success; all return empty success without the override)

### F-144. Celo pays the maximum gas refund to a CIP-64 transaction whose main call refills a fee-currency slot that the debit cleared

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: any account. Networks: Celo.

What differs on the network: On Ethereum the refund counter cannot be negative when a transaction ends. On Celo, the CIP-64 debit can clear a slot before the main call runs. The main call prices its SSTOREs against values from before the debit, so refilling that slot takes back a clearing refund that was never counted. The counter ends negative. celo-reth v1.0.10 passes it unchanged to set_final_refund, which casts it to u64 and caps it at one fifth of gas used. The celo-kona main branch clamps the counter to zero only from Karst on.

How it meets the code: A sender pays fees in a fee currency, and the debit takes its balance of that currency to exactly zero. The sender's main call then credits the sender's balance slot in the same currency again, for example through a transfer to itself from another holder or a take of that currency from the PoolManager. That SSTORE carries the -4,800 take-back, and the transaction ends with a negative counter. The handler's refund step on v1.0.10 does nothing to the counter (handler.rs:1213-1215 at the tag), and revm then grants a refund of gas_used / 5. The refund is applied after execution is complete. It changes no opcode result and no return data. Gas readings inside the main call do differ from Ethereum, because SSTOREs on slots the debit wrote are priced as already written (verifier's correction); the PoolManager forwards all gas and never branches on a GAS value (src/types/Currency.sol:48 and 73, src/libraries/Hooks.sol:134). So no line of src/ computes or decides anything differently, and the PoolManager's balances and deltas are the same as they would be on Ethereum.

What is at risk: Only the fee: the sender pays up to 20 percent less gas, and the base fee and tip recipients receive that much less. No PoolManager funds, LP positions or ERC-6909 claims are affected.

What it takes: The sender pays in a registered fee currency, the debit empties the sender's balance slot, and the main call refills it. The fork is before Karst, which is the case on Celo today.

What can be done: None needed for the PoolManager. Celo's clamp at Karst removes it.

Second reviewer's corrections: The mechanism says the CIP-64 path changes no GAS reading inside the call. That holds for the refund wrap. The celo-kona main comment at handler.rs lines 1295-1297 also says that main-call SSTOREs on slots the debit wrote stay dirty-priced, so the main call can be over- or undercharged by up to about 2,800 gas per such slot, which does shift GAS readings inside execution. src/ does not branch on remaining gas, so the conclusion stands. The actor value unprivileged is correct and severity info is correct.

Code: `src/PoolManager.sol:104-114`, `src/PoolManager.sol:291-297`.

Sources:

- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/celo-org/celo-kona/refs/tags/celo/reth-v1.0.10/crates/celo-revm/src/handler.rs (v1.0.10 refund() only forwards to the OP handler, with no clamp (lines 1213-1215))
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/celo-org/celo-kona/main/crates/celo-revm/src/handler.rs (main clamps a negative counter to zero from Karst; its comment and tests describe the wrap to the cap before Karst)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/celo-org/revm/bd28f9420c250df2a5c4a6d8103b51ae06b5c222/crates/interpreter/src/gas.rs (set_final_refund casts refunded() to u64 and takes min with gas_used / 5 (lines 199-205))

### F-149. A transaction that failed validation in Base's block builder halted every exit on Base for 116 minutes in June 2026, and the fault is fixed in every builder and node handler read

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: freeze. Who can trigger it: any account. Networks: Base.

What differs on the network: Ethereum clients drop the journal of a transaction that fails validation. Base's builder kept it, so the warm accounts and slots of the excluded transaction leaked into the next one.

How it meets the code: On 25 June 2026 an invalid transaction reached the builder of block 47806543 and failed validation. The builder did not clear the journal, the next valid transaction ran against the dirty access set and was charged different gas, and the receipts root of the sealed block differed from what validators computed. Validators rejected the block and the chain stopped at 47806542 for 116 minutes, and for 20 minutes on 26 June. Any account can send a transaction that fails validation in the builder; the PR's tests use a sender with code and a stale nonce. Because validation fails before any frame runs, no TSTORE of the failed transaction existed, so the PoolManager's lock, deltas and synced currency (src/libraries/Lock.sol, src/libraries/CurrencyDelta.sol, src/libraries/CurrencyReserves.sol) could not have been carried into the next transaction. Had a leak reached state, validators would have rejected that block too. The fix adds discard_tx to the non-deposit branch of catch_error (base/base PR 3806), present in v1.5.6 at handler.rs:385-387 and in v1.6.0-rc.3; op-revm, which the other six chains and op-rbuilder use, discards the journal on both the transaction error and other error paths, and revm's inspector run path calls the same catch_error.

What is at risk: While halted no PoolManager exit on Base could be included, so every liquidity provider and claim holder on Base was frozen for the duration. No funds were lost and no wrong state was accepted.

What it takes: A builder whose error path keeps the journal. None of the builds read today has that fault.

What can be done: None needed in core. Deployers on single-sequencer chains should expect temporary halts triggered by public transactions and keep withdrawals free of deadlines shorter than such halts.

Second reviewer's corrections: The postmortem describes the bad transaction as failing during execution; that no frame ran is inferred from PR 3806's tests, not stated by Base. The actor is right for the historical event: a stale nonce transaction is something any account can send.

Code: `src/libraries/Lock.sol:13-25`, `src/PoolManager.sol:104-114`.

Sources:

- https://www.base.org/blog/postmortem-june-25th-block-production-outage.md (root cause: stale journal after a transaction validation failure; block 47806543 rejected by validators; 116 and 20 minute halts)
- https://patch-diff.githubusercontent.com/raw/base/base/pull/3806.diff (the fix adds discard_tx in catch_error and tests EIP-3607 and nonce failures)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/base/base/v1.5.6/crates/common/evm/src/handler.rs (the running build carries the fix at lines 385-387)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ethereum-optimism/optimism/85f7c13d7470a66254081f0177fc297201c07459/rust/op-revm/src/handler.rs (the op-revm revision op-rbuilder pins discards the journal on every non-deposit error)

### F-151. X Layer runs PoolManager swaps and liquidity changes at zero gas price through two whitelisted shared carriers

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: any account. Networks: X Layer.

What differs on the network: X Layer waives the fee of a zero-priced transaction when its gasless whitelist allows the transaction's target and calldata prefix and the gas limit is within the allowance. The whitelist allows EntryPoint v0.7 for any non-empty calldata up to 30,000,000 gas and the contract at 0x7c5bee2a for any non-empty calldata up to 50,000,000 gas. Ethereum has no such rule.

How it meets the code: The executor queries getGaslessAllowance(to, input) before each transaction whose max fee per gas is zero and marks it gasless when the answer is true and the gas limit fits; it then executes the transaction normally with only the base fee check relaxed. An ordinary account can submit handleOps to EntryPoint v0.7 carrying its own user operation with zero fees, or a call to 0x7c5bee2a, and so reach the PoolManager without paying gas. In three thousand recent blocks 101 zero-priced transactions from more than 80 senders emitted PoolManager logs. The PoolManager reads neither GASPRICE nor BASEFEE (no use in src/), so every amount it computes and every settlement check in unlock (src/PoolManager.sol:104-114) is the same as in a paid transaction. The effect is cost only: dust tick filling of F-038 and other spam against pools no longer costs gas.

What is at risk: No funds move and nobody is left unable to withdraw. Griefing that F-038 records loses its gas cost on X Layer; the dust payment it needs still applies.

What it takes: Whitelist entries as read on 2026-11-26 and a sequencer that admits zero-priced transactions from any sender. The per block gasless budget and any per sender limit of the sequencer were not read, and no transaction was sent to test admission.

What can be done: None needed for funds. Pool creators who rely on gas cost as a deterrent against tick filling should not do so on X Layer.

Second reviewer's corrections: The client passes the full transaction input to getGaslessAllowance (encode_get_gasless_allowance copies all of tx.input), so "prefix" means the whole calldata. "Any non-empty calldata" is shown only for the prefixes tested. Whether 0x7c5bee2a (13,322 bytes, has an owner function; one sender is an EIP-7702 account delegated to 0xe40ccb2d) accepts calls from any account was not read; the EntryPoint v0.7 route alone carries the claim. Sequencer admission policy, the per block gasless budget and per sender limits were not read, as the finding states. The finding supersedes the cycle 11 cleared item that said no gasless path reaches the PoolManager, which tested direct targets only; that item did not concern funds.

Code: `src/PoolManager.sol:104-114`, `src/libraries/Pool.sol:166-171`.

Sources:

- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/okx/optimism/refs/tags/xlayer/v0.1.7/rust/alloy-op-evm/src/block/xlayer_gasless_contract.rs (is_gasless requires allowed == true and tx.gas_limit <= returned gasLimit; mainnet whitelist at 0x19787404b0c70021b4752028f7e3a92313885B27)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/okx/optimism/refs/tags/xlayer/v0.1.7/rust/alloy-op-evm/src/lib.rs (a gasless transaction only sets disable_base_fee for its own execution)
- https://rpc.xlayer.tech (eth_call getGaslessAllowance, out/gasless.json) (EntryPoint v0.7 allowed with 30,000,000 gas and 0x7c5bee2a with 50,000,000 gas for any non-empty prefix; PoolManager and periphery not allowed)
- https://rpc.xlayer.tech (receipts of blocks 76,927,161-76,930,160, out/gasless_scan.json) (101 mined zero-priced transactions emitted PoolManager logs, for example 0x14ba5174ce2bf8b682ac926064813eee4a9087bb449346b1500f91659056339d and 0xda396c963d30a6f9774ed5dad69e64ff0b5204432331d4accc2ed88462b42ad8)

| F-058 | owner, fee controller or chain governance | info | confirmed | BNB Smart Chain | BNB Smart Chain runs a call from the system address with its stated value and moves no balance, and no ordinary sender can be that address |
| F-089 | owner, fee controller or chain governance | info | confirmed | all 21 networks | No test fails when the unsettled delta check of unlock is removed, and under the --isolate flag of the CI no test pins the lock close or the reset of the synced currency; the source has all three statements |
| F-126 | owner, fee controller or chain governance | info | confirmed | Optimism, Unichain, Ink | The LLVM MIPS shift lowering that kona-client 1.9.0 was compiled with miscompiles 128 bit shifts by 64 to 127 under some constant folds, and a scan of the respected prestate finds no bad shape on any fall-through path |
| F-128 | owner, fee controller or chain governance | info | confirmed | Arbitrum One, Robinhood Chain, Elysium | Nitro's Stylus and JIT compilers use a Cranelift release inside the range of two October 2026 miscompile advisories, and no unprivileged account can reach either fault |
| F-131 | owner, fee controller or chain governance | info | confirmed | Optimism, Unichain, Ink | The LLVM MIPS shift lowering fault does not reach any path of the respected kona prestate, which the control flow scan and a run of its own shift handlers both show |
| F-145 | owner, fee controller or chain governance | info | confirmed | Arbitrum One, Robinhood Chain | Nitro v3.13.0 carries dormant Amsterdam execution rules that a future ArbOS version would switch on, and the PoolManager bytecode has no instruction they reinterpret |
| F-158 | owner, fee controller or chain governance | info | confirmed | Elysium | Elysium's announced ArbOS upgrade adds a HyperCore read precompile and a callback predeploy whose addresses and call shapes are not yet published |

## Where the evidence is

- `FINDINGS.md`: every finding in full, including the out-of-scope ones.
- `CLEARED.md`: the 2473 checks that came out safe, with the reason for each.
- `STATUS.md`: coverage per network and lens, open leads, cycle history.
- `cycles/NNN/`: the raw research and verdict files of each cycle.
